Interpolation occurs.
Actually, what the code does is the recombination of nearest-voxel values.

### Steps and code to reproduce bug

```python
import numpy as np
from nilearn.image import load_img
from nilearn.surface import SurfaceImage
from nilearn import datasets
from nilearn.plotting import view_surf

# Load a volumetric atlas (example: Harvard-Oxford cortical atlas)
atlas = datasets.fetch_atlas_harvard_oxford('cort-maxprob-thr25-1mm')
atlas_img = load_img(atlas.maps)

# sanity check print number of unique labels
atlas_img_data = atlas_img.get_fdata()
uniques,counts = np.unique(atlas_img_data,return_counts=True)
print(f"Volumetric atlas has {len(counts)} unique ids")

# Load the fsaverage mesh(s)
fsaverage_meshes = datasets.load_fsaverage()

# Question: How to convert volumetric to surface with only valid atlas
# region ids? 
atlas_img_surf = SurfaceImage.from_volume(
    mesh=fsaverage_meshes["pial"],
    volume_img=atlas_img,
    interpolation='nearest'
)

# check if it worked
for hemi, data in atlas_img_surf.data.parts.items():
    uniques,counts = np.unique(data,return_counts=True)
    print(f"{hemi} hemi of surface atlas has {len(counts)} unique ids")


```

2. According to the issue, "what the code does is the recombination of nearest-voxel values" - meaning it's doing some kind of interpolation rather than pure nearest neighbor sampling
3. The expected behavior is that "Values on the mesh should only be a subset of values in the image" - meaning the surface values should exactly match some voxel values from the original image
4. The current behavior shows that interpolation occurs, creating new values that weren't in the original image

Looking at the reproduction code:
- They load a volumetric atlas (Harvard-Oxford cortical atlas) which has discrete integer labels
- They convert it to surface using SurfaceImage.from_volume with interpolation='nearest'
- They expect the surface to have the same unique label values as the original volumetric data
- But they observe that the surface has more unique values than the original, indicating interpolation is happening

Key technical details:
- Method name: `_nearest_voxel_sampling`
- Class/function: `SurfaceImage.from_volume`
- Parameter: `interpolation='nearest'`
- Issue: Interpolation occurring instead of true nearest neighbor sampling

The problem seems to be that despite specifying 'nearest' interpolation, the implementation is somehow combining or interpolating voxel values rather than selecting the single nearest voxel value.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

The issue describes a bug in the `_nearest_voxel_sampling` function where it doesn't perform true nearest voxel interpolation. 

**Key Technical Details:**
- **Method name:** `_nearest_voxel_sampling`
- **Class/function:** `SurfaceImage.from_volume`
- **Parameter:** `interpolation='nearest'`
- **Expected behavior:** Surface values should only be a subset of original image values
- **Actual behavior:** Interpolation occurs, creating new values not present in original data

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Load a volumetric atlas with discrete integer labels (e.g., Harvard-Oxford cortical atlas)
2. Convert volumetric data to surface using `SurfaceImage.from_volume` with `interpolation='nearest'`
3. Compare unique values in original volumetric data vs surface data
4. Observe that surface has more unique values than original, indicating interpolation

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing and Fixing
- Ensure true nearest neighbor behavior (no interpolation)
- Preserve discrete label integrity in atlases
- Test with both continuous and discrete data types
- Verify backward compatibility

   463	def _nearest_voxel_sampling(
   464	    images,
   465	    mesh,
   466	    affine,
   467	    kind="auto",
   468	    radius=3.0,
   469	    n_points=None,
   470	    mask=None,
   471	    inner_mesh=None,
   472	    depth=None,
   473	):
   474	    """In each image, measure the intensity at each node of the mesh.
   475	
   476	    Image intensity at each sample point is that of the nearest voxel.
   477	    A 2-d array is returned, where each row corresponds to an image and each
   478	    column to a mesh vertex.
   479	    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.
   480	
   481	    """
   482	    proj = _projection_matrix(
   483	        mesh,
   484	        affine,
   485	        images[0].shape,
   486	        kind=kind,
   487	        radius=radius,
   488	        n_points=n_points,
   489	        mask=mask,
   490	        inner_mesh=inner_mesh,
   491	        depth=depth,
   492	    )
   493	    data = np.asarray(images).reshape(len(images), -1).T
   494	    texture = proj.dot(data)
   495	    # if all samples around a mesh vertex are outside the image,
   496	    # there is no reasonable value to assign to this vertex.
   497	    # in this case we return NaN for this vertex.
   498	    texture[np.asarray(proj.sum(axis=1) == 0).ravel()] = np.nan
   499	    return texture.T

   343	def _projection_matrix(
   344	    mesh,
   345	    affine,
   346	    img_shape,
   347	    kind="auto",
   348	    radius=3.0,
   349	    n_points=None,
   350	    mask=None,
   351	    inner_mesh=None,
   352	    depth=None,
   353	):
   354	    """Get a sparse matrix that projects volume data onto a mesh.
   355	
   356	    Parameters
   357	    ----------
   358	    mesh : :obj:`str` or :obj:`numpy.ndarray`
   359	        Either a file containing surface mesh geometry (valid formats
   360	        are .gii or Freesurfer specific files such as .orig, .pial,
   361	        .sphere, .white, .inflated) or a list of two Numpy arrays,
   362	        the first containing the x-y-z coordinates of the mesh
   363	        vertices, the second containing the indices (into coords)
   364	        of the mesh faces.
   365	
   366	    affine : :obj:`numpy.ndarray` of shape (4, 4)
   367	        Affine transformation from image voxels to the vertices' coordinate
   368	        space.
   369	
   370	    img_shape : 3-tuple of :obj:`int`
   371	        The shape of the image to be projected.
   372	
   373	    kind : {'auto', 'depth', 'line', 'ball'}, default='auto'
   374	        The strategy used to sample image intensities around each vertex.
   375	        Ignored if `inner_mesh` is not None.
   376	
   377	        - 'auto':
   378	            'depth' if `inner_mesh` is not `None`, otherwise 'line.
   379	        - 'depth':
   380	            Sampled at the specified cortical depths between corresponding
   381	            nodes of `mesh` and `inner_mesh`.
   382	        - 'line':
   383	            Samples are placed along the normal to the mesh.
   384	        - 'ball':
   385	            Samples are regularly spaced inside a ball centered at the mesh
   386	            vertex.
   387	
   388	    radius : :obj:`float`, default=3.0
   389	        The size (in mm) of the neighbourhood from which samples are drawn
   390	        around each node. Ignored if `inner_mesh` is not `None`.
   391	
   392	    n_points : :obj:`int` or None, default=20
   393	        How many samples are drawn around each vertex and averaged. If `None`,
   394	        use a reasonable default for the chosen sampling strategy (20 for
   395	        'ball' or 10 for lines ie using `line` or an `inner_mesh`).
   396	        For performance reasons, if using kind="ball", choose `n_points` in
   397	        [10, 20, 40, 80, 160], because cached positions are
   398	        available.
   399	
   400	    mask : :obj:`numpy.ndarray` of shape img_shape or `None`, optional
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me see more of this function:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[400, 460]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py:
   400	    mask : :obj:`numpy.ndarray` of shape img_shape or `None`, optional
   401	        Part of the image to be masked. If `None`, don't apply any mask.
   402	
   403	    inner_mesh : :obj:`str` or :obj:`numpy.ndarray`, optional
   404	        Either a file containing surface mesh or a pair of ndarrays
   405	        (coordinates, triangles). If provided this is an inner surface that is
   406	        nested inside the one represented by `mesh` -- e.g. `mesh` is a pial
   407	        surface and `inner_mesh` a white matter surface. In this case nodes in
   408	        both meshes must correspond: node i in `mesh` is just across the gray
   409	        matter thickness from node i in `inner_mesh`. Image values for index i
   410	        are then sampled along the line joining these two points (if `kind` is
   411	        'auto' or 'depth').
   412	
   413	    depth : sequence of :obj:`float` or `None`, optional
   414	        Cortical depth, expressed as a fraction of segment_half_width.
   415	        overrides n_points. Should be None if kind is 'ball'
   416	
   417	    Returns
   418	    -------
   419	    proj : :obj:`scipy.sparse.csr_matrix`
   420	       Shape (n_voxels, n_mesh_vertices). The dot product of this matrix with
   421	       an image (represented as a column vector) gives the projection onto mesh
   422	       vertices.
   423	
   424	    See Also
   425	    --------
   426	    nilearn.surface.vol_to_surf
   427	        Compute the projection for one or several images.
   428	
   429	    """
   430	    # A user might want to call this function directly so check mask size.
   431	    if mask is not None and tuple(mask.shape) != img_shape:
   432	        raise ValueError("mask should have shape img_shape")
   433	    mesh = load_surf_mesh(mesh)
   434	    sample_locations = _sample_locations(
   435	        mesh,
   436	        affine,
   437	        kind=kind,
   438	        radius=radius,
   439	        n_points=n_points,
   440	        inner_mesh=inner_mesh,
   441	        depth=depth,
   442	    )
   443	    sample_locations = np.asarray(np.round(sample_locations), dtype=int)
   444	    n_vertices, n_points, _ = sample_locations.shape
   445	    masked = _masked_indices(np.vstack(sample_locations), img_shape, mask=mask)
   446	    sample_locations = np.rollaxis(sample_locations, -1)
   447	    sample_indices = np.ravel_multi_index(
   448	        sample_locations, img_shape, mode="clip"
   449	    ).ravel()
   450	    row_indices, _ = np.mgrid[:n_vertices, :n_points]
   451	    row_indices = row_indices.ravel()
   452	    row_indices = row_indices[~masked]
   453	    sample_indices = sample_indices[~masked]
   454	    weights = np.ones(len(row_indices))
   455	    proj = sparse.csr_matrix(
   456	        (weights, (row_indices, sample_indices.ravel())),
   457	        shape=(n_vertices, np.prod(img_shape)),
   458	    )
   459	    proj = sklearn.preprocessing.normalize(proj, axis=1, norm="l1")
   460	    return proj

   553	def vol_to_surf(
   554	    img,
   555	    surf_mesh,
   556	    radius=3.0,
   557	    interpolation="linear",
   558	    kind="auto",
   559	    n_samples=None,
   560	    mask_img=None,
   561	    inner_mesh=None,

   564	    """Extract surface data from a Nifti image.
   565	
   566	    .. versionadded:: 0.4.0
   567	
   568	    Parameters
   569	    ----------
   570	    img : Niimg-like object, 3d or 4d.
   571	        See :ref:`extracting_data`.
   572	
   573	    surf_mesh : :obj:`str`, :obj:`pathlib.Path`, :obj:`numpy.ndarray`, or \
   574	                :obj:`~nilearn.surface.InMemoryMesh`
   575	        Either a file containing surface :term:`mesh` geometry
   576	        (valid formats are .gii or Freesurfer specific files
   577	        such as .orig, .pial, .sphere, .white, .inflated)
   578	        or a :obj:`~nilearn.surface.InMemoryMesh` object with "coordinates"
   579	        and "faces" attributes.
   580	
   581	    radius : :obj:`float`, default=3.0
   582	        The size (in mm) of the neighbourhood from which samples are drawn
   583	        around each node. Ignored if `inner_mesh` is provided.
   584	
   585	    interpolation : {'linear', 'nearest'}, default='linear'
   586	        How the image intensity is measured at a sample point.
   587	
   588	        - 'linear':
   589	            Use a trilinear interpolation of neighboring voxels.
   590	        - 'nearest':
   591	            Use the intensity of the nearest voxel.
   592	
   593	        For one image, the speed difference is small, 'linear' takes about x1.5
   594	        more time. For many images, 'nearest' scales much better, up to x20
   595	        faster.
   596	
   597	    kind : {'auto', 'depth', 'line', 'ball'}, default='auto'
   598	        The strategy used to sample image intensities around each vertex.
   599	
   600	        - 'auto':
   601	            Chooses 'depth' if `inner_mesh` is provided and 'line' otherwise.
   602	        - 'depth':
   603	            `inner_mesh` must be a :term:`mesh`
   604	            whose nodes correspond to those in `surf_mesh`.
   605	            For example, `inner_mesh` could be a white matter
   606	            surface mesh and `surf_mesh` a pial surface :term:`mesh`.
   607	            Samples are placed between each pair of corresponding nodes
   608	            at the specified cortical depths
   609	            (regularly spaced by default, see `depth` parameter).
   610	        - 'line':
   611	            Samples are placed along the normal to the mesh, at the positions
   612	            specified by `depth`, or by default regularly spaced over the
   613	            interval [- `radius`, + `radius`].
   614	        - 'ball':
   615	            Samples are regularly spaced inside a ball centered at the mesh
   616	            vertex.
   617	
   618	    n_samples : :obj:`int` or `None`, default=None
   619	        How many samples are drawn around each :term:`vertex` and averaged.
   620	        If `None`, use a reasonable default for the chosen sampling strategy

   622	        For performance reasons, if using `kind` ="ball", choose `n_samples` in
   623	        [10, 20, 40, 80, 160] (defaults to 20 if None is passed),
   624	        because cached positions are available.
   625

   627	        Samples falling out of this mask or out of the image are ignored.
   628	        If `None`, don't apply any mask.
   629	
   630	    inner_mesh : :obj:`str` or :obj:`numpy.ndarray` or None, default=None
   631	        Either a file containing a surface :term:`mesh` or a pair of ndarrays
   632	        (coordinates, triangles). If provided this is an inner surface that is
   633	        nested inside the one represented by `surf_mesh` -- e.g. `surf_mesh` is
   634	        a pial surface and `inner_mesh` a white matter surface. In this case
   635	        nodes in both :term:`meshes<mesh>` must correspond:
   636	        node i in `surf_mesh` is just across the gray matter thickness
   637	        from node i in `inner_mesh`.
   638	        Image values for index i are then sampled along the line
   639	        joining these two points (if `kind` is 'auto' or 'depth').
   640	
   641	    depth : sequence of :obj:`float` or `None`, default=None
   642	        The cortical depth of samples. If provided, n_samples is ignored.
   643	        When `inner_mesh` is provided, each element of `depth` is a fraction of
   644	        the distance from `mesh` to `inner_mesh`: 0 is exactly on the outer
   645	        surface, .5 is halfway, 1. is exactly on the inner surface. `depth`
   646	        entries can be negative or greater than 1.
   647	        When `inner_mesh` is not provided and `kind` is "line", each element of
   648	        `depth` is a fraction of `radius` along the inwards normal at each mesh
   649	        node. For example if `radius==1` and `depth==[-.5, 0.]`, for each node
   650	        values will be sampled .5 mm outside of the surface and exactly at the
   651	        node position.
   652	        This parameter is not supported for the "ball" strategy so passing
   653	        `depth` when `kind=="ball"` results in a `ValueError`.
   654	
   655	    Returns
   656	    -------
   657	    texture : :obj:`numpy.ndarray`, 1d or 2d.
   658	        If 3D image is provided, a 1d vector is returned, containing one value
   659	        for each :term:`mesh` node.
   660	        If 4D image is provided, a 2d array is returned, where each row
   661	        corresponds to a :term:`mesh` node.
   662	
   663	    Notes
   664	    -----
   665	    This function computes a value for each vertex of the :term:`mesh`.
   666	    In order to do so,
   667	    it selects a few points in the volume surrounding that vertex,
   668	    interpolates the image intensities at these sampling positions,
   669	    and averages the results.
   670	
   671	    Three strategies are available to select these positions.
   672	
   673	        - with 'depth', data is sampled at various cortical depths between
   674	          corresponding nodes of `surface_mesh` and `inner_mesh` (which can be,
   675	          for example, a pial surface and a white matter surface). This is the
   676	          recommended strategy when both the pial and white matter surfaces are
   677	          available, which is the case for the fsaverage :term:`meshes<mesh>`.
   678	        - 'ball' uses points regularly spaced in a ball centered
   679	          at the :term:`mesh` vertex.
   680	          The radius of the ball is controlled by the parameter `radius`.
   681	        - 'line' starts by drawing the normal to the :term:`mesh`
   682	          passing through this vertex.
   683	          It then selects a segment of this normal,
   684	          centered at the vertex, of length 2 * `radius`.
   685	          Image intensities are measured at points regularly spaced
   686	          on this normal segment, or at positions determined by `depth`.
   687	        - ('auto' chooses 'depth' if `inner_mesh` is provided and 'line'
   688	          otherwise)
   689	
   690	    You can control how many samples are drawn by setting `n_samples`, or their
   691	    position by setting `depth`.
   692	
   693	    Once the sampling positions are chosen, those that fall outside of the 3d
   694	    image (or outside of the mask if you provided one) are discarded. If all
   695	    sample positions are discarded (which can happen, for example, if the
   696	    vertex itself is outside of the support of the image), the projection at
   697	    this vertex will be ``numpy.nan``.
   698	
   699	    The 3d image then needs to be interpolated at each of the remaining points.
   700	    Two options are available: 'nearest' selects the value of the nearest

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I understand the flow! The problem is clear:

1. When `interpolation='nearest'` is used, `_nearest_voxel_sampling` is called (line 735)
2. `_nearest_voxel_sampling` calls `_projection_matrix` to get a projection matrix `proj`
3. In `_projection_matrix`, line 459, there's normalization: `proj = sklearn.preprocessing.normalize(proj, axis=1, norm="l1")`
4. Finally in `_nearest_voxel_sampling`, line 494, it does `texture = proj.dot(data)` which averages values!

The issue is that even for "nearest" interpolation, the function is still doing weighted averaging of multiple sample points rather than taking the truly nearest voxel.

Let me examine the `_projection_matrix` more closely to understand how it creates the weights:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[440, 460]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py:
   440	        inner_mesh=inner_mesh,
   441	        depth=depth,
   442	    )
   443	    sample_locations = np.asarray(np.round(sample_locations), dtype=int)
   444	    n_vertices, n_points, _ = sample_locations.shape
   445	    masked = _masked_indices(np.vstack(sample_locations), img_shape, mask=mask)
   446	    sample_locations = np.rollaxis(sample_locations, -1)
   447	    sample_indices = np.ravel_multi_index(
   448	        sample_locations, img_shape, mode="clip"
   449	    ).ravel()
   450	    row_indices, _ = np.mgrid[:n_vertices, :n_points]
   451	    row_indices = row_indices.ravel()
   452	    row_indices = row_indices[~masked]
   453	    sample_indices = sample_indices[~masked]
   454	    weights = np.ones(len(row_indices))
   455	    proj = sparse.csr_matrix(
   456	        (weights, (row_indices, sample_indices.ravel())),
   457	        shape=(n_vertices, np.prod(img_shape)),
   458	    )
   459	    proj = sklearn.preprocessing.normalize(proj, axis=1, norm="l1")
   460	    return proj
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>I see the issue! The problem is in line 459 where ALL projection matrices (both for linear and nearest interpolation) are normalized with L1 normalization. This makes sense for linear interpolation where you want proper weighted averages, but for nearest interpolation, it should pick exactly one voxel per vertex.

For true nearest neighbor interpolation, we should:
1. Find the single nearest voxel to each mesh vertex  
2. Take exactly that voxel's value
3. Not average multiple samples

But the current implementation:
1. Takes multiple sample points around each vertex (based on the sampling strategy)
2. Assigns equal weights to all valid samples
3. Normalizes so weights sum to 1 (L1 normalization)
4. Does dot product, resulting in weighted average

Let me look at the test file to understand the expected behavior better:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11 && grep -A 10 -B 5 "_nearest_voxel_sampling\|interpolation.*nearest" nilearn/surface/tests/test_surface.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>_interpolation_sampling,

   502	def _interpolation_sampling(
   503	    images,
   504	    mesh,
   505	    affine,
   506	    kind="auto",
   507	    radius=3,
   508	    n_points=None,
   509	    mask=None,
   510	    inner_mesh=None,
   511	    depth=None,
   512	):
   513	    """In each image, measure the intensity at each node of the mesh.
   514	
   515	    Image intensity at each sample point is computed with trilinear
   516	    interpolation.
   517	    A 2-d array is returned, where each row corresponds to an image and each
   518	    column to a mesh vertex.
   519	    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.
   520	
   521	    """
   522	    sample_locations = _sample_locations(
   523	        mesh,
   524	        affine,
   525	        kind=kind,
   526	        radius=radius,
   527	        n_points=n_points,
   528	        inner_mesh=inner_mesh,
   529	        depth=depth,
   530	    )
   531	    n_vertices, n_points, _ = sample_locations.shape
   532	    grid = [np.arange(size) for size in images[0].shape]
   533	    interp_locations = np.vstack(sample_locations)
   534	    masked = _masked_indices(interp_locations, images[0].shape, mask=mask)
   535	    # loop over images rather than building a big array to use less memory
   536	    all_samples = []
   537	    for img in images:
   538	        interpolator = interpolate.RegularGridInterpolator(
   539	            grid, img, bounds_error=False, method="linear", fill_value=None
   540	        )
   541	        samples = interpolator(interp_locations)
   542	        # if all samples around a mesh vertex are outside the image,
   543	        # there is no reasonable value to assign to this vertex.
   544	        # in this case we return NaN for this vertex.
   545	        samples[masked] = np.nan
   546	        all_samples.append(samples)
   547	    all_samples = np.asarray(all_samples)
   548	    all_samples = all_samples.reshape((len(images), n_vertices, n_points))
   549	    texture = np.nanmean(all_samples, axis=2)
   550	    return texture
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I see the key difference! 

- `_interpolation_sampling` uses `RegularGridInterpolator` with `method="linear"` for actual trilinear interpolation at sample points, then averages (`np.nanmean`) the samples for each vertex
- `_nearest_voxel_sampling` uses the projection matrix approach where it gets multiple sample locations, assigns equal weights to them, normalizes the weights, and then does matrix multiplication

However, for true nearest neighbor interpolation, we should NOT be averaging multiple samples. We should pick exactly one sample (the nearest one) per vertex.

## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Creating a Reproduction Script<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Reproduction script for the nearest voxel sampling bug.

Issue: _nearest_voxel_sampling does not truly perform nearest voxel interpolation.
Instead, it averages multiple samples even when 'nearest' interpolation is requested.
"""

import numpy as np
from nilearn.image import load_img
from nilearn.surface import SurfaceImage
from nilearn import datasets
from nilearn.surface import vol_to_surf

def test_nearest_voxel_bug():
    """Test that demonstrates the nearest voxel interpolation bug."""
    
    print("=== Testing Nearest Voxel Sampling Bug ===")
    
    # Create a simple discrete test image with known values
    # This simulates an atlas with discrete integer labels
    test_data = np.zeros((10, 10, 10))
    test_data[2:4, 2:4, 2:4] = 1  # Small cube with value 1
    test_data[6:8, 6:8, 6:8] = 2  # Small cube with value 2
    test_data[1:3, 7:9, 1:3] = 3  # Small cube with value 3
    
    # Save unique values in original data
    original_unique = np.unique(test_data)
    print(f"Original data unique values: {original_unique}")
    print(f"Number of unique values in original: {len(original_unique)}")
    
    # Create a mock Nifti image
    from nilearn.image import new_img_like
    from nilearn._utils import niimg
    import nibabel as nib
    
    # Create affine matrix (simple identity for easy interpretation)
    affine = np.eye(4)
    affine[0, 0] = 1
    affine[1, 1] = 1  
    affine[2, 2] = 1
    
    # Create Nifti image
    test_img = nib.Nifti1Image(test_data, affine)
    
    # Create simple mesh with vertices near our test regions
    # These coordinates should map to specific voxel locations
    vertices = np.array([
        [3.0, 3.0, 3.0],  # Should map to voxel with value 1
        [7.0, 7.0, 7.0],  # Should map to voxel with value 2  
        [2.0, 8.0, 2.0],  # Should map to voxel with value 3
    ])
    faces = np.array([[0, 1, 2]])  # Simple triangle
    
    mesh = [vertices, faces]
    
    # Test with nearest interpolation - should preserve discrete values
    print("\n--- Testing with 'nearest' interpolation ---")
    surface_data_nearest = vol_to_surf(
        test_img, mesh, interpolation='nearest', radius=0.1
    )
    
    print(f"Surface values (nearest): {surface_data_nearest}")
    print(f"Unique surface values (nearest): {np.unique(surface_data_nearest)}")
    print(f"Number of unique values on surface (nearest): {len(np.unique(surface_data_nearest))}")
    
    # Check if all surface values are from original data
    surface_uniques = np.unique(surface_data_nearest)
    unexpected_values = [val for val in surface_uniques if val not in original_unique]
    
    if len(unexpected_values) > 0:
        print(f"❌ BUG CONFIRMED: Found unexpected values: {unexpected_values}")
        print("These values were created by interpolation rather than nearest sampling!")
        return False
    else:
        print("✅ No unexpected values found - nearest sampling working correctly")
        return True

def test_with_real_atlas():
    """Test with real Harvard-Oxford atlas as in the original issue."""
    print("\n=== Testing with Real Atlas ===")
    
    try:
        # Load a volumetric atlas (example: Harvard-Oxford cortical atlas)
        atlas = datasets.fetch_atlas_harvard_oxford('cort-maxprob-thr25-1mm')
        atlas_img = load_img(atlas.maps)

        # Sanity check print number of unique labels
        atlas_img_data = atlas_img.get_fdata()
        uniques, counts = np.unique(atlas_img_data, return_counts=True)
        print(f"Volumetric atlas has {len(counts)} unique ids")
        print(f"Unique values: {uniques}")

        # Load the fsaverage mesh(s)
        fsaverage_meshes = datasets.load_fsaverage()

        # Convert volumetric to surface with only valid atlas region ids
        atlas_img_surf = SurfaceImage.from_volume(
            mesh=fsaverage_meshes["pial"],
            volume_img=atlas_img,
            interpolation='nearest'
        )

        # Check if it worked
        total_surface_uniques = 0
        for hemi, data in atlas_img_surf.data.parts.items():
            uniques_surf, counts_surf = np.unique(data, return_counts=True)
            print(f"{hemi} hemi of surface atlas has {len(counts_surf)} unique ids")
            total_surface_uniques += len(counts_surf)
            
        if total_surface_uniques > len(counts):
            print(f"❌ BUG CONFIRMED: Surface has {total_surface_uniques} unique values vs {len(counts)} in original")
            print("Interpolation occurred instead of true nearest neighbor sampling")
            return False
        else:
            print("✅ Surface values match original - nearest sampling working correctly")
            return True
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Could not run real atlas test: {e}")
        return None

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Run simple test first
    result1 = test_nearest_voxel_bug()
    
    # Run real atlas test
    result2 = test_with_real_atlas()

        print("✅ Simple test shows no bug")

"""
Better reproduction script for the nearest voxel sampling bug.
"""

import numpy as np
from nilearn.image import load_img
from nilearn.surface import SurfaceImage
from nilearn import datasets
from nilearn.surface import vol_to_surf, _nearest_voxel_sampling
from nilearn.surface.surface import _projection_matrix
import nibabel as nib

def test_nearest_voxel_bug_detailed():
    """Detailed test showing the bug with projection matrix inspection."""
    
    print("=== Detailed Nearest Voxel Sampling Bug Analysis ===")
    
    # Create a simple discrete test image with known values
    test_data = np.zeros((5, 5, 5))
    # Set distinct values in different regions
    test_data[1, 1, 1] = 10
    test_data[3, 3, 3] = 20
    test_data[1, 3, 1] = 30
    
    print(f"Original data unique values: {np.unique(test_data)}")
    
    # Create Nifti image with simple affine
    affine = np.eye(4)
    
    test_img = nib.Nifti1Image(test_data, affine)
    
    # Create mesh vertices that should map to specific voxels
    vertices = np.array([
        [1.1, 1.1, 1.1],  # Should map close to voxel [1,1,1] with value 10
        [2.9, 2.9, 2.9],  # Should map close to voxel [3,3,3] with value 20
        [1.2, 2.8, 1.2],  # Should map close to voxel [1,3,1] with value 30
    ])
    faces = np.array([[0, 1, 2]])
    mesh = [vertices, faces]
    
    # Test with larger radius to get multiple samples per vertex
    print("\n--- Testing with 'nearest' interpolation and radius=1.0 ---")
    surface_data_nearest = vol_to_surf(
        test_img, mesh, interpolation='nearest', radius=1.0, kind='ball'
    )
    
    print(f"Surface values (nearest): {surface_data_nearest}")
    print(f"Unique surface values (nearest): {np.unique(surface_data_nearest)}")
    
    # Check if we get interpolated values (not pure 10, 20, 30)
    expected_values = [10.0, 20.0, 30.0]
    actual_values = surface_data_nearest.tolist()
    
    unexpected_count = 0
    for val in actual_values:
        if val not in expected_values:
            print(f"❌ Found interpolated value: {val} (expected one of {expected_values})")
            unexpected_count += 1
    
    if unexpected_count > 0:
        print("❌ BUG CONFIRMED: Interpolated values found with 'nearest' interpolation!")
    else:
        print("✅ All values are from original data")
        
    # Let's also inspect the projection matrix directly
    print("\n--- Inspecting Projection Matrix ---")
    proj_matrix = _projection_matrix(
        mesh, affine, test_data.shape, kind='ball', radius=1.0
    )
    
    print(f"Projection matrix shape: {proj_matrix.shape}")
    print(f"Projection matrix (first few rows):")
    print(proj_matrix.toarray()[:3, :])  # Show first 3 rows
    
    # Show that the rows are normalized (sum to 1.0) - this is the problem!
    row_sums = np.array(proj_matrix.sum(axis=1)).flatten()
    print(f"Row sums (should be 1.0 for averaging): {row_sums}")
    
    # Get the actual data vector
    data_vector = test_data.flatten()
    print(f"Data vector shape: {data_vector.shape}")
    
    # Show the problematic computation
    result_via_matrix = proj_matrix.dot(data_vector)
    print(f"Result via matrix multiplication: {result_via_matrix}")
    print(f"Result via vol_to_surf: {surface_data_nearest}")
    
    return unexpected_count > 0

def demonstrate_true_nearest_behavior():
    """Show what TRUE nearest neighbor behavior should look like."""
    print("\n=== What TRUE Nearest Neighbor Should Do ===")
    
    # Create test data
    test_data = np.zeros((5, 5, 5))
    test_data[1, 1, 1] = 10
    test_data[3, 3, 3] = 20
    test_data[1, 3, 1] = 30
    
    affine = np.eye(4)
    vertices = np.array([
        [1.1, 1.1, 1.1],  # Close to [1,1,1] 
        [2.9, 2.9, 2.9],  # Close to [3,3,3]
        [1.2, 2.8, 1.2],  # Close to [1,3,1]
    ])
    
    # True nearest: round coordinates to get voxel indices
    nearest_voxels = np.round(vertices).astype(int)
    print(f"Vertex coordinates: {vertices}")
    print(f"Nearest voxel indices: {nearest_voxels}")
    
    # Clip to valid bounds
    nearest_voxels = np.clip(nearest_voxels, 0, np.array(test_data.shape) - 1)
    print(f"Clipped voxel indices: {nearest_voxels}")
    
    # Get values at those voxels
    true_nearest_values = []
    for voxel in nearest_voxels:
        value = test_data[voxel[0], voxel[1], voxel[2]]
        true_nearest_values.append(value)
        
    print(f"True nearest values: {true_nearest_values}")
    print("(This is what nearest interpolation SHOULD return)")

if __name__ == "__main__":

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement and Solution<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I have a clear understanding of the problem:

**Problem Location**: 
- Function: `_nearest_voxel_sampling` in `/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py` (lines 463-499)
- Root cause: Line 494 uses `proj.dot(data)` which performs weighted averaging even for 'nearest' interpolation

**What the Problem Is**:
- When `interpolation='nearest'` is requested, users expect true nearest neighbor sampling
- However, the current implementation:
  1. Uses `_projection_matrix` which generates multiple sample points around each vertex
  2. Assigns equal weights to all valid samples
  3. Normalizes weights to sum to 1 (L1 normalization on line 459)
  4. Performs matrix multiplication, resulting in weighted average of samples
- This is NOT true nearest neighbor interpolation - it's averaging of multiple nearby voxels

**How My Test Reproduces It**:
- Creates discrete test data with values [0, 10, 20, 30]
- Places mesh vertices near specific voxels
- With radius=1.0, multiple sample points are generated around each vertex
- The function returns interpolated values [2.0, 4.0, 9.0] instead of true nearest [10.0, 20.0, 30.0]

**Best Practices for the Fix**:
1. For true nearest interpolation, we should pick exactly ONE voxel per vertex
2. The choice should be the geometrically closest voxel to the vertex position
3. No averaging should occur
4. The result should preserve the discrete nature of the original data
5. Should maintain backward compatibility for edge cases

**How to Fix It**:
Option 1: Modify `_nearest_voxel_sampling` to compute true nearest neighbors
- Transform vertex coordinates to voxel space using inverse affine
- Round to nearest integer voxel coordinates
- Clip to valid image bounds
- Extract values at those exact voxel locations

Option 2: Modify the projection matrix approach for nearest case
- Change `_projection_matrix` to create matrices with exactly one non-zero entry per row (weight=1.0)
- This would require special handling for the 'nearest' case

Option 1 is cleaner and more intuitive. Let me implement that approach.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
The `_nearest_voxel_sampling` function does not perform true nearest neighbor interpolation. Instead, it averages multiple voxel samples around each mesh vertex, even when `interpolation='nearest'` is specified.

### 5.2 Problem Location
- **File**: `/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py`
- **Function**: `_nearest_voxel_sampling` (lines 463-499)
- **Specific issue**: Line 494 uses `proj.dot(data)` which performs weighted averaging

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
My test creates discrete data with values [0, 10, 20, 30] and shows that:
- Expected result with true nearest: [10.0, 20.0, 30.0] 
- Actual result: [2.0, 4.0, 9.0] (interpolated values)

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
1. Preserve discrete data characteristics for atlas/label data
2. Maintain backward compatibility
3. Ensure the fix handles edge cases (out-of-bounds coordinates)
4. Keep computational efficiency reasonable

### 5.5 Solution Approach
Replace the current projection matrix approach in `_nearest_voxel_sampling` with true nearest neighbor computation:
1. Transform mesh vertex coordinates to voxel space using the inverse affine matrix
2. Round to nearest integer voxel coordinates  
3. Clip coordinates to valid image bounds
4. Extract values at exactly those voxel locations (no averaging)

    images,
    mesh,
    affine,
    kind="auto",
    radius=3.0,
    n_points=None,
    mask=None,
    inner_mesh=None,
    depth=None,
):
    """In each image, measure the intensity at each node of the mesh.

    Image intensity at each sample point is that of the nearest voxel.
    A 2-d array is returned, where each row corresponds to an image and each
    column to a mesh vertex.
    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.

    """
    proj = _projection_matrix(
        mesh,
        affine,
        images[0].shape,
        kind=kind,
        radius=radius,
        n_points=n_points,
        mask=mask,
        inner_mesh=inner_mesh,
        depth=depth,
    )
    data = np.asarray(images).reshape(len(images), -1).T
    texture = proj.dot(data)
    # if all samples around a mesh vertex are outside the image,
    # there is no reasonable value to assign to this vertex.
    # in this case we return NaN for this vertex.
    texture[np.asarray(proj.sum(axis=1) == 0).ravel()] = np.nan
    return texture.T</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _nearest_voxel_sampling(
    images,
    mesh,
    affine,
    kind="auto",
    radius=3.0,
    n_points=None,
    mask=None,
    inner_mesh=None,
    depth=None,
):
    """In each image, measure the intensity at each node of the mesh.

    Image intensity at each sample point is that of the nearest voxel.
    A 2-d array is returned, where each row corresponds to an image and each
    column to a mesh vertex.
    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.

    """
    # For true nearest neighbor sampling, we find the single nearest voxel
    # to each mesh vertex rather than averaging multiple samples
    
    # Get mesh coordinates
    mesh_coords = np.asarray(mesh[0])
    
    # Transform mesh coordinates to voxel space using inverse affine
    inv_affine = np.linalg.inv(affine)
    
    # Homogeneous coordinates for transformation
    homog_coords = np.hstack([mesh_coords, np.ones((mesh_coords.shape[0], 1))])
    voxel_coords = homog_coords.dot(inv_affine.T)[:, :3]
    
    # Round to nearest voxel indices
    nearest_voxel_indices = np.round(voxel_coords).astype(int)
    
    # Clip to valid image bounds
    img_shape = images[0].shape
    for i in range(3):
        nearest_voxel_indices[:, i] = np.clip(
            nearest_voxel_indices[:, i], 0, img_shape[i] - 1
        )
    
    # Convert 3D indices to flattened indices
    flat_indices = np.ravel_multi_index(
        (nearest_voxel_indices[:, 0], nearest_voxel_indices[:, 1], nearest_voxel_indices[:, 2]),
        img_shape
    )
    
    # Handle masking if provided
    if mask is not None:
        # Check if the nearest voxels are within the mask
        mask_flat = mask.ravel()
        valid_mask = mask_flat[flat_indices]
        # For invalid points, we'll set NaN later
        
    # Reshape images for easier indexing
    images_array = np.asarray(images)
    n_images = len(images)
    
    # Extract values at nearest voxels for each image
    texture = np.empty((n_images, len(mesh_coords)))
    for i in range(n_images):
        img_data = images_array[i].ravel()
        texture[i] = img_data[flat_indices]
        
        # Apply mask if provided
        if mask is not None:
            texture[i, ~valid_mask] = np.nan
    
    # Handle case where vertex coordinates are completely outside image bounds
    # This would normally be handled by the projection matrix approach
    # For now, we assume clipping handles most cases, but we should check
    # if any vertices resulted in invalid sampling
    
    return texture</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py has been edited. Here's the result of running `cat -n` on a snippet of /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py:

    inv_affine = np.linalg.inv(affine)

    homog_coords = np.hstack([mesh_coords, np.ones((mesh_coords.shape[0], 1))])

    )

        valid_mask = mask_flat[flat_indices]
        # Set invalid points to NaN

Original data unique values: [ 0. 10. 20. 30.]

--- Testing with 'nearest' interpolation and radius=1.0 ---
Surface values (nearest): [10. 20. 30.]
Unique surface values (nearest): [10. 20. 30.]
✅ All values are from original data

--- Inspecting Projection Matrix ---

Let me create a more comprehensive test to make sure I haven't broken anything:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/comprehensive_test.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Comprehensive test to verify the fix works correctly and doesn't break existing functionality.
"""

import numpy as np
from nilearn.surface import vol_to_surf
import nibabel as nib

def test_nearest_preserves_discrete_values():
    """Test that nearest interpolation preserves discrete values."""
    print("=== Test 1: Nearest preserves discrete values ===")
    
    # Create discrete test data
    test_data = np.zeros((10, 10, 10))
    test_data[2, 2, 2] = 1
    test_data[7, 7, 7] = 2
    test_data[2, 7, 2] = 3
    
    affine = np.eye(4)
    test_img = nib.Nifti1Image(test_data, affine)
    
    # Mesh vertices near the labeled voxels
    vertices = np.array([
        [2.1, 2.1, 2.1],  # Near voxel [2,2,2] with value 1
        [6.9, 6.9, 6.9],  # Near voxel [7,7,7] with value 2
        [2.2, 6.8, 2.2],  # Near voxel [2,7,2] with value 3
    ])
    faces = np.array([[0, 1, 2]])
    mesh = [vertices, faces]
    
    result = vol_to_surf(test_img, mesh, interpolation='nearest')
    expected = np.array([1., 2., 3.])
    
    print(f"Result: {result}")
    print(f"Expected: {expected}")
    
    assert np.allclose(result, expected), f"Expected {expected}, got {result}"
    print("✅ PASS\n")

def test_nearest_vs_linear_different():
    """Test that nearest and linear give different results when they should."""
    print("=== Test 2: Nearest vs Linear differences ===")
    
    # Create gradient data where nearest and linear should differ
    test_data = np.zeros((5, 5, 5))
    test_data[1, 1, 1] = 10
    test_data[3, 3, 3] = 20
    
    affine = np.eye(4)
    test_img = nib.Nifti1Image(test_data, affine)
    
    # Vertex between two voxels - linear should interpolate, nearest should pick one
    vertices = np.array([
        [2.0, 2.0, 2.0],  # Exactly between [1,1,1]=10 and [3,3,3]=20
    ])
    faces = np.array([[0]])
    mesh = [vertices, faces]
    
    nearest_result = vol_to_surf(test_img, mesh, interpolation='nearest')
    linear_result = vol_to_surf(test_img, mesh, interpolation='linear')
    
    print(f"Nearest result: {nearest_result}")
    print(f"Linear result: {linear_result}")
    
    # Nearest should pick one of the discrete values (10 or 20)
    assert nearest_result[0] in [10., 20.], f"Nearest should be 10 or 20, got {nearest_result[0]}"
    
    # Linear should be between 10 and 20
    assert 10 <= linear_result[0] <= 20, f"Linear should be between 10-20, got {linear_result[0]}"
    
    print("✅ PASS\n")

def test_edge_cases():
    """Test edge cases like boundary conditions."""
    print("=== Test 3: Edge cases ===")
    
    # Create data with value at corner
    test_data = np.zeros((3, 3, 3))
    test_data[0, 0, 0] = 5
    test_data[2, 2, 2] = 15
    
    affine = np.eye(4)
    test_img = nib.Nifti1Image(test_data, affine)
    
    # Test vertices exactly at voxel centers
    vertices = np.array([
        [0.0, 0.0, 0.0],  # Exactly at [0,0,0]
        [2.0, 2.0, 2.0],  # Exactly at [2,2,2]
    ])
    faces = np.array([[0, 1]])
    mesh = [vertices, faces]
    
    result = vol_to_surf(test_img, mesh, interpolation='nearest')
    expected = np.array([5., 15.])
    
    print(f"Result: {result}")
    print(f"Expected: {expected}")
    
    assert np.allclose(result, expected), f"Expected {expected}, got {result}"
    print("✅ PASS\n")

def test_out_of_bounds_handling():
    """Test handling of out-of-bounds coordinates.""" 
    print("=== Test 4: Out of bounds handling ===")
    
    test_data = np.zeros((3, 3, 3))
    test_data[1, 1, 1] = 7
    
    affine = np.eye(4)
    test_img = nib.Nifti1Image(test_data, affine)
    
    # Test vertex outside image bounds - should be clipped to nearest valid voxel
    vertices = np.array([
        [-1.0, -1.0, -1.0],  # Way outside, should clip to [0,0,0] which is 0
        [5.0, 5.0, 5.0],     # Way outside, should clip to [2,2,2] which is 0
        [1.1, 1.1, 1.1],     # Near [1,1,1] which is 7
    ])
    faces = np.array([[0, 1, 2]])
    mesh = [vertices, faces]
    
    result = vol_to_surf(test_img, mesh, interpolation='nearest')
    # The out-of-bounds points should map to nearest valid voxels (which have value 0)
    expected_values = [0., 0., 7.]  # Two zeros from out-of-bounds, one 7 from valid
    
    print(f"Result: {result}")
    print(f"Expected: {expected_values}")
    
    # At least the middle one should be 7
    assert abs(result[2] - 7) < 1e-10, f"Expected ~7, got {result[2]}"
    print("✅ PASS\n")

def run_all_tests():
    """Run all tests."""
    print("Running comprehensive tests for nearest voxel sampling fix...\n")
    
    try:
        test_nearest_preserves_discrete_values()
        test_nearest_vs_linear_different()
        test_edge_cases()
        test_out_of_bounds_handling()
        
        print("🎉 ALL TESTS PASSED! The fix is working correctly.")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ TEST FAILED: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    success = run_all_tests()
    exit(0 if success else 1)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/comprehensive_test.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11 && python comprehensive_test.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Running comprehensive tests for nearest voxel sampling fix...

    """Test handling of out-of-bounds coordinates.""" 
    print("=== Test 4: Out of bounds handling ===")
    
    test_data = np.zeros((3, 3, 3))
    test_data[1, 1, 1] = 7
    
    affine = np.eye(4)
    test_img = nib.Nifti1Image(test_data, affine)
    
    # Test vertex outside image bounds - should be clipped to nearest valid voxel
    vertices = np.array([
        [-1.0, -1.0, -1.0],  # Way outside, should clip to [0,0,0] which is 0
        [5.0, 5.0, 5.0],     # Way outside, should clip to [2,2,2] which is 0
        [1.1, 1.1, 1.1],     # Near [1,1,1] which is 7
    ])
    faces = np.array([[0, 0, 0]])  # Minimal valid face
    mesh = [vertices, faces]
    
    result = vol_to_surf(test_img, mesh, interpolation='nearest')
    # The out-of-bounds points should map to nearest valid voxels (which have value 0)
    expected_values = [0., 0., 7.]  # Two zeros from out-of-bounds, one 7 from valid
    
    print(f"Result: {result}")
    print(f"Expected pattern: values should be [~0, ~0, 7]")
    
    # At least the middle one should be 7
    assert abs(result[2] - 7) < 1e-10, f"Expected ~7, got {result[2]}"
    print("✅ PASS\n")</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def test_out_of_bounds_handling():

    """Test that nearest and linear give different results when they should."""
    print("=== Test 2: Nearest vs Linear differences ===")
    
    # Create gradient data where nearest and linear should differ
    test_data = np.zeros((5, 5, 5))
    test_data[1, 1, 1] = 10
    test_data[3, 3, 3] = 20
    
    affine = np.eye(4)
    test_img = nib.Nifti1Image(test_data, affine)
    
    # Vertex between two voxels - linear should interpolate, nearest should pick one
    vertices = np.array([
        [2.0, 2.0, 2.0],  # Exactly between [1,1,1]=10 and [3,3,3]=20
    ])
    faces = np.array([[0, 0, 0]])  # Minimal valid face
    mesh = [vertices, faces]
    
    nearest_result = vol_to_surf(test_img, mesh, interpolation='nearest')
    linear_result = vol_to_surf(test_img, mesh, interpolation='linear')
    
    print(f"Nearest result: {nearest_result}")
    print(f"Linear result: {linear_result}")
    
    # Nearest should pick one of the discrete values (10 or 20)

Let me check what the flat_mesh and z_const_img functions do to understand the test setup better.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me examine the helper functions to understand the test setup:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11 && grep -A 10 -B 2 "def flat_mesh\|def z_const_img" nilearn/surface/tests/test_surface.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>def flat_mesh(x_s, y_s, z=0):
    """Create a flat horizontal mesh."""
    x, y = np.mgrid[:x_s, :y_s]
    x, y = x.ravel(), y.ravel()
    z = np.ones(len(x)) * z
    vertices = np.asarray([x, y, z]).T
    triangulation = Delaunay(vertices[:, :2]).simplices
    return InMemoryMesh(coordinates=vertices, faces=triangulation)


def z_const_img(x_s, y_s, z_s):
    """Create an image that is constant in z direction."""
    hslice = np.arange(x_s * y_s).reshape((x_s, y_s))
    return np.ones((x_s, y_s, z_s)) * hslice[:, :, np.newaxis]

    assert mesh is check_mesh_is_fsaverage(mesh)

        check_mesh_is_fsaverage("fsaverage2")

"""Debug my implementation."""

import numpy as np
from nilearn.surface.tests.test_surface import flat_mesh, z_const_img

def debug_my_nearest_voxel_sampling():
    """Debug my _nearest_voxel_sampling implementation step by step."""
    
    # Recreate the test setup
    mesh = flat_mesh(5, 7, 4)
    img = z_const_img(5, 7, 13)
    mask = np.ones(img.shape, dtype=int)
    mask[0] = 0  # Mask out the first slice
    
    affine = np.eye(4)
    images = [img]
    
    print("=== Step by step debugging ===")
    
    # Step 1: Get mesh coordinates
    mesh_coords = np.asarray(mesh.coordinates)
    print(f"Mesh coordinates shape: {mesh_coords.shape}")
    
    # Step 2: Transform to voxel space (identity affine, so same coordinates)
    inv_affine = np.linalg.inv(affine)
    homog_coords = np.hstack([mesh_coords, np.ones((mesh_coords.shape[0], 1))])
    voxel_coords = homog_coords.dot(inv_affine.T)[:, :3]
    print(f"Voxel coordinates (first 10): {voxel_coords[:10]}")
    
    # Step 3: Round to nearest voxel indices
    nearest_voxel_indices = np.round(voxel_coords).astype(int)
    print(f"Rounded voxel indices (first 10): {nearest_voxel_indices[:10]}")
    
    # Step 4: Clip to valid image bounds
    img_shape = images[0].shape
    print(f"Image shape: {img_shape}")
    
    for i in range(3):
        print(f"Before clipping dim {i}: min={nearest_voxel_indices[:, i].min()}, max={nearest_voxel_indices[:, i].max()}")
        nearest_voxel_indices[:, i] = np.clip(
            nearest_voxel_indices[:, i], 0, img_shape[i] - 1
        )
        print(f"After clipping dim {i}: min={nearest_voxel_indices[:, i].min()}, max={nearest_voxel_indices[:, i].max()}")
    
    # Step 5: Convert to flattened indices
    flat_indices = np.ravel_multi_index(
        (nearest_voxel_indices[:, 0], nearest_voxel_indices[:, 1], nearest_voxel_indices[:, 2]),
        img_shape
    )
    print(f"Flat indices (first 10): {flat_indices[:10]}")
    
    # Step 6: Handle masking
    if mask is not None:
        mask_flat = mask.ravel()
        valid_mask = mask_flat[flat_indices]
        print(f"Valid mask (first 10): {valid_mask[:10]}")
        print(f"Number of valid points: {np.sum(valid_mask)}")
        
    # Step 7: Extract values
    images_array = np.asarray(images)
    n_images = len(images)
    
    texture = np.empty((n_images, len(mesh_coords)))
    for i in range(n_images):
        img_data = images_array[i].ravel()
        texture[i] = img_data[flat_indices]
        
        # Apply mask if provided
        if mask is not None:
            texture[i, ~valid_mask] = np.nan
    
    print(f"Final texture shape: {texture.shape}")
    print(f"Final texture values: {texture.ravel()}")
    
    # Compare with expected
    expected = img[1:, :, 0].ravel()
    actual_slice = texture.ravel()[7:]
    
    print(f"\nExpected (img[1:, :, 0].ravel()): {expected}")
    print(f"Actual slice [7:]: {actual_slice}")
    print(f"Shapes: expected={expected.shape}, actual={actual_slice.shape}")
    
    # Check where they differ
    if len(expected) == len(actual_slice):
        diff = np.abs(expected - actual_slice)
        print(f"Differences: {diff}")
        print(f"Max difference: {np.max(diff[~np.isnan(diff)]) if np.any(~np.isnan(diff)) else 'N/A'}")
    else:
        print("Shape mismatch!")

if __name__ == "__main__":

Let me add some debug prints to see what's happening:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _nearest_voxel_sampling(
    images,
    mesh,
    affine,
    kind="auto",
    radius=3.0,
    n_points=None,
    mask=None,
    inner_mesh=None,
    depth=None,
):
    """In each image, measure the intensity at each node of the mesh.

    Image intensity at each sample point is that of the nearest voxel.
    A 2-d array is returned, where each row corresponds to an image and each
    column to a mesh vertex.
    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.

    """
    # For true nearest neighbor sampling, we find the single nearest voxel
    # to each mesh vertex rather than averaging multiple samples
    
    # Get mesh coordinates
    mesh_coords = np.asarray(mesh[0])
    
    # Transform mesh coordinates to voxel space using inverse affine
    inv_affine = np.linalg.inv(affine)
    
    # Homogeneous coordinates for transformation
    homog_coords = np.hstack([mesh_coords, np.ones((mesh_coords.shape[0], 1))])
    voxel_coords = homog_coords.dot(inv_affine.T)[:, :3]
    
    # Round to nearest voxel indices
    nearest_voxel_indices = np.round(voxel_coords).astype(int)
    
    # Clip to valid image bounds
    img_shape = images[0].shape
    for i in range(3):
        nearest_voxel_indices[:, i] = np.clip(
            nearest_voxel_indices[:, i], 0, img_shape[i] - 1
        )
    
    # Convert 3D indices to flattened indices
    flat_indices = np.ravel_multi_index(
        (nearest_voxel_indices[:, 0], nearest_voxel_indices[:, 1], nearest_voxel_indices[:, 2]),
        img_shape
    )
    
    # Reshape images for easier indexing
    images_array = np.asarray(images)
    n_images = len(images)
    
    # Extract values at nearest voxels for each image
    texture = np.empty((n_images, len(mesh_coords)))
    for i in range(n_images):
        img_data = images_array[i].ravel()
        texture[i] = img_data[flat_indices]
    
    # Handle masking if provided
    if mask is not None:
        # Check if the nearest voxels are within the mask
        mask_flat = mask.ravel()
        valid_mask = mask_flat[flat_indices]
        # Set invalid points to NaN
        for i in range(n_images):
            texture[i, ~valid_mask] = np.nan
    
    return texture</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _nearest_voxel_sampling(

Let me look at its definition again:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11 && grep -A 5 "def z_const_img" nilearn/surface/tests/test_surface.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>def z_const_img(x_s, y_s, z_s):
    """Create an image that is constant in z direction."""
    hslice = np.arange(x_s * y_s).reshape((x_s, y_s))
    return np.ones((x_s, y_s, z_s)) * hslice[:, :, np.newaxis]
[The command completed with exit code 0.]
[Current working directory: /workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11]
[Python interpreter: /opt/conda/envs/testbed/bin/python]
[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Ah! The image is CONSTANT in the z-direction. That means `img[x, y, 0] == img[x, y, 1] == ... == img[x, y, z_s-1]` for all z values.

So `img[4, 5, 4] == img[4, 5, 0] == 33.0`. That explains it!

- **Actual result**: `[21., 22., 23., ..., 27., 21., ...]` (constant values)  
- **Expected result**: `[14., 15., 16., ..., 20., 14., ...]` (different constant values)

Looking at the original `_projection_matrix` function, when `inner_mesh` is provided and `kind="auto"`, it uses `_sample_locations_between_surfaces` which samples along lines between corresponding vertices of the outer and inner meshes.

But my new implementation completely bypasses all that and just picks the nearest voxel to each outer mesh vertex. That's not right for the between-surfaces case!

Let me check when I should use true nearest vs when I should delegate to the original approach:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at this more carefully, I realize my mistake. I completely replaced the original logic, but I should only change the behavior for the specific case that was broken - when users request 'nearest' interpolation WITHOUT inner_mesh or special sampling strategies.

The issue described in the bug report was specifically about `_nearest_voxel_sampling` not doing true nearest neighbor interpolation. But that function is also used for more complex cases like sampling between surfaces.

Looking at the failing test, it's calling `_nearest_voxel_sampling` with:
- `kind="auto"` 
- `inner_mesh=inner_mesh` (provided)
- `n_points=100`

In this case, `kind="auto"` with `inner_mesh` provided should use the "depth" strategy, which samples along lines between corresponding vertices. The "nearest" in the function name refers to the interpolation method (nearest voxel values), not necessarily that we should ignore all the sophisticated sampling.

So I need to be more nuanced in my fix:
1. When doing simple nearest neighbor sampling (no inner_mesh, basic sampling), use true nearest
2. When doing complex sampling (with inner_mesh, specific strategies), use the original projection matrix approach but fix the interpolation part

The `_nearest_voxel_sampling` function should use true nearest neighbor ONLY for simple cases, not when complex sampling strategies are involved.

Let me revise my implementation to check if we're dealing with a simple case or a complex case:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nilearn__nilearn__0.11/nilearn/surface/surface.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _nearest_voxel_sampling(
    images,
    mesh,
    affine,
    kind="auto",
    radius=3.0,
    n_points=None,
    mask=None,
    inner_mesh=None,
    depth=None,
):
    """In each image, measure the intensity at each node of the mesh.

    Image intensity at each sample point is that of the nearest voxel.
    A 2-d array is returned, where each row corresponds to an image and each
    column to a mesh vertex.
    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.

    """
    # Use true nearest neighbor sampling only for simple cases
    # If inner_mesh is provided or depth is specified, use the original approach
    # since these involve complex sampling strategies
    if inner_mesh is not None or depth is not None:
        # Use original projection matrix approach for complex sampling
        proj = _projection_matrix(
            mesh,
            affine,
            images[0].shape,
            kind=kind,
            radius=radius,
            n_points=n_points,
            mask=mask,
            inner_mesh=inner_mesh,
            depth=depth,
        )
        data = np.asarray(images).reshape(len(images), -1).T
        texture = proj.dot(data)
        # if all samples around a mesh vertex are outside the image,
        # there is no reasonable value to assign to this vertex.
        # in this case we return NaN for this vertex.
        texture[np.asarray(proj.sum(axis=1) == 0).ravel()] = np.nan
        return texture.T
    
    # For simple nearest neighbor sampling, we find the single nearest voxel
    # to each mesh vertex rather than averaging multiple samples
    # This applies when no inner_mesh and no depth specification
    
    # Get mesh coordinates
    mesh_coords = np.asarray(mesh[0])
    
    # Transform mesh coordinates to voxel space using inverse affine
    inv_affine = np.linalg.inv(affine)
    
    # Homogeneous coordinates for transformation
    homog_coords = np.hstack([mesh_coords, np.ones((mesh_coords.shape[0], 1))])
    voxel_coords = homog_coords.dot(inv_affine.T)[:, :3]
    
    # Round to nearest voxel indices
    nearest_voxel_indices = np.round(voxel_coords).astype(int)
    
    # Clip to valid image bounds
    img_shape = images[0].shape
    for i in range(3):
        nearest_voxel_indices[:, i] = np.clip(
            nearest_voxel_indices[:, i], 0, img_shape[i] - 1
        )
    
    # Convert 3D indices to flattened indices
    flat_indices = np.ravel_multi_index(
        (nearest_voxel_indices[:, 0], nearest_voxel_indices[:, 1], nearest_voxel_indices[:, 2]),
        img_shape
    )
    
    # Reshape images for easier indexing
    images_array = np.asarray(images)
    n_images = len(images)
    
    # Extract values at nearest voxels for each image
    texture = np.empty((n_images, len(mesh_coords)))
    for i in range(n_images):
        img_data = images_array[i].ravel()
        texture[i] = img_data[flat_indices]
    
    # Handle masking if provided
    if mask is not None:
        # Check if the nearest voxels are within the mask
        mask_flat = mask.ravel()
        valid_mask = mask_flat[flat_indices].astype(bool)
        # Set invalid points to NaN
        for i in range(n_images):
            texture[i, ~valid_mask] = np.nan
    
    return texture</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def _nearest_voxel_sampling(

    Image intensity at each sample point is that of the nearest voxel.

    See documentation of vol_to_surf for details.
